A pattern is anchored at both ends as XSD requires, and several `xs:pattern` facets in one restriction are alternatives. A restriction of a restriction can only tighten a bound or a length and cannot add a pattern to one that has it. **Occurrence**: `minOccurs`/`maxOccurs` on an element make an array (`minOccurs="2" maxOccurs="5"` is an array of 2 to 5; `minOccurs="0"` makes the field optional); on a `sequence` they must be 1. **`#text` is never required**, since an empty element is a valid empty string. Documentation in an `xs:annotation` becomes the description of the type it is in; `appinfo` is refused. Every unknown element or attribute, and every excluded one (`choice`, `all`, `any`, `group`, `complexContent`, `extension` of a complex type, `nillable`, `default`, `fixed`, `mixed`, `key`, `import`, `list`, `union`, ...), fails with its name, where it was written, and why.
- **`builtin:xsd`** (`metaschema::xsd_meta`) is an XSD, written in the subset and lowered like any schema, with the XML Schema namespace as its target and `xs:` for both its own types and the built-ins. A name in that namespace is one of its own types if the schema declares such a type, and a built-in otherwise; that rule is only for this one case. It describes the elements and attributes the subset accepts (`schema`, `element`, `complexType`, `sequence`, `simpleContent`/`extension`, `attribute`, `simpleType`/`restriction` with its six facets, `annotation`/`documentation`) and flags the rest as undeclared. Lowering stays the authority: it checks what the structure cannot say (`maxOccurs` below `minOccurs`, a missing type name).
- **XSD tabs** (`Calc::add_xsd_sheet`, `SchemaFormat`, `schema_format`): a schema tab holds the XSD as the tree the meta-schema types, is edited, validated and given formulas like any tab, and lowers (`xsd::lower_node`) every time it is edited; a document that does not lower says why and leaves the tabs bound to it as they were, as for JSON Schema. The prefixes in `type="xs:string"` are resolved against the namespaces declared on the document's root (the tab's `XmlDocument`).
- **`XMLPATH(query, node)`** is a subset of XPath 1.0 over the same tree (`python/xmlpath.rs`): the steps `name`, `*`, `@name`, `@*`, `text()` and `.`, with `/` and `//`; predicates with a position, `last()`, comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not()`, a path alone (it exists), and `position()`, `count()`, `contains()` and `starts-with()`. **The node it is given is the context**, and a leading `/` means the same, so `/line/sku` is `line/sku` with the tab as the root element; given a repeated element it runs on each occurrence (`XMLPATH('.[qty > 1]/sku', Orders.line)`). It returns the matches in document order, once each, as the values and proxies a formula would get by walking there. Like `JSONPATH` it reads all of `node`, so a query over a whole tab is circular with the formulas on it (`Orders.line`, not `Orders`, in the tests). The parent axis, other axes, unions and arithmetic are refused with a message. Comparisons follow XPath: a number against anything compares as numbers, a set of nodes is true if any member is.
- **Not done in this part, and why.**
  - **The XML forms of the field commands** (add an element or an attribute, rename, delete, reorder among elements and among attributes separately) and *Change the schema from a data tab* for an XSD: the commands of 4d find a type in the schema document by a JSON pointer and edit `properties` and `required`; the XSD document needs its own way to find and edit a type. *(Done later, 2026-10-28: see the decisions for the XML field commands.)*
  - **Comments in the window** and the comment commands: the model, the reading and the writing are done; the cell comment, the context menu items and the indicator wait for the workbook layer's look at the window.
  - **Inference of a schema from XML data** and the import and export dialogs belong to the workbook layer.
  - **Files:** `data.xml` in the archive and the manifest wait for the workbook layer. Nothing yet decides, from a file, that a tab is XML; a tab is XML because its schema is an XSD.
  - **The sample workbook** is still all JSON.
  - **Formats the subset leaves out** stay out, as the design says: mixed content (refused in a schema; in data, text next to child elements is kept as `#text` and flagged as undeclared; from 0.5.0 it is a mixed type, see XML Schemas and namespaces, 0.5.0), more than one namespace, `xsi:` attributes.
- **Tests.** In `gossia-core`: 11 for the XML layer, 20 for XSD lowering (what lowers, each excluded construct by name, the shape of the document, facets), 23 for the data adapter (reading, invalid data, unknown content, comments, writing, namespaces, simple content, dates, numbers), and 4 for the XSD meta-schema (it lowers; the sample reads as a tree it accepts and that lowers the same as the text; it flags what is outside the subset; a schema tree written as XML is the schema again). In `gossia-calc`: 9 for the `XMLPATH` parser and evaluator and 10 through the real engine (XML tabs, attributes and formulas on them, `XMLPATH` and its dependencies and errors, editing the XSD relowering the tab, a schema that does not lower, the field commands on an XML tab, writing the tab back as XML).

**Decisions made while building step 7a** (the view selector and the generated Default form)
- **The view selector** is the **View > Show as** submenu, with a radio item for each view: **Data tree**, **Default** and **Raw**. The choice is per tab (`GossiaApp::views`, a tab not in the map shows its tree), and a tab keeps it while the window is open; saving it in the workbook waits for the workbook layer, like the other view state. Only the Default form exists yet; named forms come in 7b and will be listed between Default and Raw. *(Since 2026-10-28 the order is **Data tree** and **Raw**, which every tab has, then a line, then the forms, Default first.)*
- **The Raw view** (`raw.rs`) shows the tab's data as the document it is stored as, pretty printed: JSON for a JSON Schema tab, XML (with the declaration, prefix, namespaces and comments the tab keeps) for an XSD tab. The text is read only but can be selected and copied, and a **Copy** button copies the whole document. **A paste (Ctrl+V) in this view is imported as a file is** (see Import and export, "Pasting"); one choice of the import dialog **replaces the whole document**, if the pasted text parses and has no problems against the tab's schema; otherwise it is refused, nothing changes, and the reason is shown in red above the text (the parse error with its line, or the number of problems with the first six, each with its path). An accepted paste is one undoable edit of the tab (the XML document details of the pasted text are kept and are not part of the undo). Formulas stay where they are and are calculated against the pasted data. The clipboard cannot be read by a button (egui only receives it as a paste), so the paste is the keyboard command, as the hint beside the Copy button says. The control channel has `paste` for it. The view has no formula bar, as the form has none.
- **Switching** first enters what is being typed (the cell being edited, and the text of every field of the tab that was typed and not yet committed). A refused entry keeps the user in the view and says why. Going from the form to the tree selects, and opens the tree down to, the node whose field had the focus; going to the form scrolls to the field bound to the selected node. Only that exact node is looked for: the *nearest control above* the design promises for a node that has no control of its own (an item the stepper is not on) is not done yet. *(Done later: see the decisions for the gaps in forms.)*
- **What the form view leaves out of the window:** the formula bar, the schema bar and the table pane, since they belong to the tree. The arrow-key movement of the tree is off in the form, where the keyboard works as it does in any form (Tab and Shift+Tab between fields). The status bar, the trust banner, the issues panel and the menu stay. Undo and redo (the menu, and the keys while no text field has the focus) are the workbook's own.
- **The generated form is drawn from the schema each time** (`form.rs`, one `Painter` over the `TypeSpec` and the data). The root is a heading, the schema's `title` or the tab's name; an object is a group titled by its field name; the help text of the schema is on the label (and under the title of a group); a required field has a red mark. Fields of an object that the schema does not define are not drawn (the tree shows them). Data that is not of the field's type (an array where a number should be) is shown as its summary and is read only.
- **Editors by type:** a toggle for a boolean; a dropdown for an enumeration (with *(none)* when the field is optional); a text field for numbers, dates, date-times and strings, with a hint for the form of the text, and several lines for a string whose `maxLength` is over 200. **A date is a text field with the hint `yyyy-mm-dd`, not a date picker**: the picker is a separate `egui_extras` feature, and a field that validates as typed is what the design asks for first; the picker can be added without changing anything else. *(A button beside the field that opens a calendar was added later: see the decisions for the gaps in forms.)* An error value shows as its code in red with the message on hover and cannot be edited.
- **Reading and writing.** A field shows the tab's current value, so it follows every edit, recalculation and undo. **A text is a draft only while it is being typed:** each change is checked as it is typed (`entry::classify`, so with the same rules and messages as the grid); a text that does not fit stays in the field, outlined in red with the reason under it and on hover, and is not written; a text that fits is entered when Enter is pressed or the field loses the focus, as one ordinary undoable edit, and Escape drops the draft. A toggle or a choice is entered at once. An empty text clears the node (an optional field becomes absent, as in the grid), and typing into an absent optional field creates it. A field also gets the outline when the data in the tab does not fit, as the grid does.
- **A form has no formulas.** A text that starts with `=` is refused in a field of a number, a date or the like, with a message that says so; in a text field it is the text. A field bound to a node that has a formula, or is inside the result of one, is read only, tinted as computed, with the small *fx* marker (the formula, or what computes it, on hover); the waiting marker shows while it is out of date. Formulas are edited in the property page of 7b and in the tree until then.
- **Repeaters.** An array whose items are scalars, or objects with no array or map inside, is a table with a header row and a narrow row header (`[0]`), and *New* in the title. **The row commands *Move up*, *Move down* and *Delete row* are in the context menu of the row** (right click on the row header or on the background of a row), not buttons, which took too much width. A click on the header of a column of objects sorts the array by it, as in the tabular view (a real, undoable reorder, with the same refusal rules). **Filtering, hiding, and showing the counts of hidden and filtered rows are not in the form's tables yet**; the tabular view of the tree has them. *(Added later: see the decisions for the gaps in forms.)* Any other array is stacked: one item at a time with *Previous item*, *Next item*, "3 of 17", *Delete*, and *New*, which goes to the new item. A move swaps neighbours, since a form's table has no hidden rows. An array that is computed (a formula on the array node) cannot be added to, deleted from, sorted or reordered.
- **Absent containers.** An optional or missing object is a label with *Add*, which makes it with its required fields filled in, as the tree's defaults do. An absent array is made by *New*.
- **Numbers are right aligned** in their fields, in the form's tables as well (the grid of the data tree and the tabular view still show them to the left; they can follow).
- **Icons** are the Phosphor Icons font (MIT, public and free to use and ship), taken unchanged from the `egui-phosphor` 0.14.0 crate, which cannot be used itself since it is built for an earlier egui; the licence notice is in `crates/gossia-app/assets/PHOSPHOR-NOTICE.txt`. It is added to egui on the first frame as a font family of its own (so it never replaces a text glyph) and takes effect on the next, and an icon button draws a letter on the first frame. A button's name (for a screen reader and the tests) and its tip are what it does, not the glyph.
- **Maps** are a list of entries (the key, the value's editor, *Delete entry*) and a field for the key of a new entry with *Add entry*. An empty key or one that is there already is refused with a message.
- **Control channel:** `view` (`{"name": "Default"}` or `"Data tree"`), `status` reports `view`, and `enter` types into the selected node's field when the view is a form (so one scenario drives either view).
- **Not done in 7a, and why.** Saving the view and the stepper position in the workbook (the workbook layer); the marks of an untrusted workbook on the fields (the grid has `#UNTRUSTED!`; a form field shows the stored value without the mark yet); filters and hiding in the form's tables; a date picker; XML forms of the field and the comment commands (as for the tree). *(The marks, the filters and hiding, and the date picker were done later: see the decisions for the gaps in forms.)*
- **Tests.** `tests/forms.rs`: 16 that drive the window (the selector and the view kept per tab; a committed field being an undoable tab edit; a value that does not fit staying in the field with the reason, the view refusing to change until it is corrected; no formulas; computed fields; the toggle and the choice; an absent optional field; the table with new, delete and move; sorting; maps; the selection carried back to the tree; the control commands; the Raw view showing JSON and XML, a conforming paste being accepted and undone, and a paste that does not conform, or is not JSON or XML, being refused with the reasons) and two new visual snapshot tests with three images (the Default form of Orders and of Prices, and the Raw view). Other snapshot images changed because the tab row lost the label and the menu has a new item; the Linux ones are made on the runner.

**Decisions made while building step 7b** (named forms, layout mode and the property page)
- **The design** is `gossia_core::form`: `FormDesign {version, title, controls}`, a tree of `Control`s (field, group, repeater, label, button, separator) with a flat serde struct, validated against the `builtin:form` schema (`form_schema()`) when read; a newer `version` is refused. A control is addressed by its place in the tree (`Address`, a list of indexes), which is also how the selection is kept.
- **Default is generated until it is changed.** `generate(spec)` makes it from the schema; the first edit stores it (*materialises* it) and from then on it no longer follows the schema. Undoing back to nothing leaves it generated again. *Reset Default form...* discards the stored design. Other forms are always stored. Per tab, `FormStore` (in `gossia-calc`) maps name to design, Default first; names follow the rules of a tab name and also refuse `Raw`.
- **View > Forms** holds *New form...*, *Duplicate form...*, *Rename form...*, *Delete form...*, *Reset Default form...* and the *Edit form* check box. New and Duplicate start from the form shown and show the copy. A dialog that is refused keeps its reason in the dialog. The view selector lists Data tree, Default, the tab's forms, Raw. A form that is gone shows Default.
- **Layout mode** (*Edit form*, also a check box above the form) draws every control disabled and selectable: click to select, a selection and hover outline, a context menu (Move up, Move down, Move into the group above, Move out of the group, Delete control). A strip of buttons adds a field, group, repeater, label, button or separator: into the selected group, after the selected control, or at the end. Layout mode has its own **Undo layout** and **Redo layout**; design edits are not in the workbook's undo history and do not change its revision.
- **The property page** is a right panel: path (a combo of what can be bound), label, help, editor (Automatic, Text, Multiline), read only, group columns (1 to 6), repeater table or stacked and its columns, label text, button label and procedure, visible-when and enabled-when, the move buttons, and the **formula** of the bound node (entered through the ordinary entry path, so undoable in the workbook). It also lists **Fields not on the form** (with *Add*) and **Broken controls** (with *Re-bind* and *Remove*). A broken control is drawn as a red placeholder that says why.
- **Not done in 7b, and why.** visible-when and enabled-when are stored and editable but not evaluated (they need the engine and trust work of 7c; done later, see the decisions for expressions on controls); buttons are drawn disabled (7c); controls are moved by menu and buttons, not drag and drop (drag and drop came later, see its decisions); stacked repeater items are drawn as generated, not designable (designable later: see the decisions for the gaps in forms); designs are not saved to a file (the workbook layer: `sheets/<Tab>/forms/<Form>.json`, the manifest and the chosen view); there is no raw JSON pane for a design yet; the confirmation of *Delete form* and *Reset* is not in layout undo; tabs cannot be renamed or deleted yet, so forms do not follow them.
- **Tests.** In `gossia-core`, 9 on the design (round trip, schema validation, tree operations, generation, broken and unplaced controls); in `gossia-calc`, 3 on the store; `form_layout_tests.rs`, 12 without a window; `tests/forms.rs`, 5 more that drive the window (a form made from the menu, a refused name, selecting and editing a label, deleting from the context menu and adding back, a broken control removed); snapshots `layout_mode_orders` and `two_column_form_orders`, and the existing form snapshots changed for the *Edit form* strip.


**Decisions made for the gaps in forms** (2026-10-30)
- **The arrow keys** (`form_nav.rs`). Each control that can take the focus is noted as it is drawn, with its rectangle and how keys leave it; after the form is drawn, an arrow key pressed (with no modifier) in one of them moves the focus. Up and Down go to the nearest row that way, then to the control in it whose left edge is closest, so fields under one another and the cells of a column match; Left and Right go to the nearest control in the same row (overlapping by at least half its height). In a text box Left and Right leave it only when the caret did not move, which is known by comparing it with the frame before, so the caret goes to the end first. Up and Down leave a one-line text box, a check box, a drop-down and a button at once; a box of several lines and a typeahead keep them. A field that is read only (computed, or set read only) is passed by, as Tab passes it: its box cannot take the focus, so stopping there left the keys with nowhere to go (found by the owner on 2026-10-30, moving right from *price* to the computed *total*). egui moves the focus with the arrow keys over the whole window; the form cancels that, so the focus stays in the form, and at its edge a key does nothing. Not in layout mode, where the keys move controls, nor while a pop-up is open.
- **The date picker** is `egui_extras`' `DatePickerButton` (its `datepicker` feature, and `jiff` for today's date), a button with a calendar icon beside the text field of a date or a date-time (by the type, or a string's format). A day picked is entered at once, as a choice is; a date-time keeps its time, or gets midnight. It opens at the date in the field, or today. Its month and year drop-downs are off (they have no accessible names) and so are the week numbers; the arrows step by day, month and year. Not on a field that is read only, masked or of several lines.
- **The untrusted mark.** A field whose node has a formula that was not run shows `#UNTRUSTED!` after it, as the grid does, with the same tooltip; a value that is out of date (something it uses has changed) is grey. Shared with the grid (`view::untrusted_mark`).
- **The nearest control above.** Going to a node from the tree, the form looks for its control; when it is not drawn, it takes the nearest node above it that has one, in the next frame. A stacked repeater first steps to the item that holds the node, and the control is looked for again (up to three frames).
- **Filters and hiding in a form's tables** share the state of the tabular view (`TableView`, by tab and array), as DESIGN.md said a repeater's table includes them: one state, so a filter set in either place shows in both and is saved with the view state. A column's title has a context menu (*Sort ascending*, *Sort descending*, *Filter...*, which opens the tabular view's filter dialog, *Clear filter*, *Hide column*) and the filter mark; a row's menu has *Hide row*. Beside the table's title: how many rows are shown, hidden and filtered, *Clear all filters*, *Reapply filter* and the *Hidden (N)* menu that shows a column or a row again. A row edited or added on the form stays shown until the filters change, as in the tabular view. A column hidden by hand is hidden on every form of the tab; the design's own hidden columns are apart. **Layout mode shows every row and column**, so that what is designed can be seen and selected.
- **Designed items.** The controls of a stacked repeater (`children`, as a group has) are the design of its items; their paths are relative to the item, and the empty path is the item. The design stays a tree of controls, so selecting, moving, dragging, adding (into the selected repeater), the property page and the layouts work in it as in a group; in layout mode the repeater is drawn as a group, showing the item its stepper is at. A repeater takes controls only once its items are designed (*Design the items*), so that one dropped in does not replace the generated look by itself; when its last control is taken out it shows the generated look again. *Fields not on the form* adds after the repeater, since its paths are whole. A field's formula is not on its property page in an item: it would be a formula of one item; a column formula on the array is that, and it is set in the data tree. A design of items that is not used (the repeater shows a table, or is inside another item) is listed under *Broken controls* with the reason.
- **Their expressions run for each item** (`ExprKey.row`, as for the cells of a column), at the control's node in that item, or at the item for a control that is not bound; the list follows the number of items. The form asks for the one of the item shown; the property page shows the first item that failed (*Item [1]: ...*).
- **Tests.** `tests/form_keys.rs` (6: Up and Down between fields entering what was typed, a table from cell to cell, passing by a computed field, the `#UNTRUSTED!` marks, picking a date and a date-time from the calendar, stepping to the item of a node), `tests/form_tables.rs` (5: a filter, a row edited under one staying shown until reapplied, the column menu's filter dialog and hiding, hiding a row, layout mode showing all), `tests/form_items.rs` (4: the item drawn by its design and its per-item visibility, expressions per item following edits and new items, *Design the items* and adding into it, selecting a control of the items in layout mode), and unit tests in `form_nav.rs` and `gossia-core`'s `form.rs`.

**Decisions made while building step 7d-3** (the Grid layout, 2026-10-30)
- **The design document** gains, on a group, `layout: "grid"`, `grid_columns` and `grid_rows` (up to 50 each), each size written `"auto"`, a number of points (`"120"`, 8 to 2000) or a share (`"2fr"`, 1 to 99); on a control, `grid_row` and `grid_column` (from 1, up to 50) and `row_span`. A grid control takes `span` columns, the same property a flow uses; its limit in the document is now 50 (a flow still takes at most its number of columns).
- **Cells** (`form::grid_cells` in `gossia-core`). A control with a row and a column is in that cell; one without either is in the next free cell, row by row, after the one placed before it, as wide as it fits. A control placed past the last column or row adds automatic ones, so the grid has the rows and columns it defines or more. Cells can be empty, and two controls placed in one cell overlap (the later on top); nothing refuses it.
- **Sizes** (`form::track_sizes`). Points are that; automatic is the size of the biggest control that takes only that row or column (known from the frame before, so a grid settles in its second frame); shares divide what the other tracks and the gaps leave of the group's width, and of its height when it has one; without a height, shared rows are automatic. The gaps are the group's spacing. A control in a column that is not automatic fills its cell, unless it has a width, which its alignment places in the cell; one in an automatic column has its own width.
- **Layout mode** draws the lines between the rows and columns, gives an empty column 40 points and an empty row 24 so there is room to drop into them, and outlines the cell under a dragged control. Dragging a control (a group by its title) and letting go puts it in that cell; just past the last row or column adds one. Dragging from the palette, or a control of another group, onto a cell puts it there, at the end of the list. Each is one change. The arrow keys and the align tools that move do not apply to a grid; *Same width* and *Same height* do.
- **The property page** of a Grid group lists its columns and rows (those it defines and those its controls add), each with *Automatic*, *Points* or *Share* and the number, and a button that removes it, with *Add column* and *Add row*; and the tab order, as a Free group does. A control in a grid shows *Cell in the grid*: the row and column it is in (typing either places it there) and the rows and columns it takes. Turning a flow into a grid keeps its arrangement: as many equal shares as it had columns, each control in the cell its flow row gave it; a Free group becomes one column in the order of its controls.
- **Tests.** `gossia-core`: cells taken and found free with spans and growth, track sizes for points, automatic and shares, the grid stored and its sizes checked. `form_free.rs` (unit): the cell under the pointer, in a gap and past the last. `tests/grid_layout.rs`: controls lined up in two shared columns; a column of points; a drag to a new row as one undo; a label from the palette dropped on an empty cell; a flow turned into a grid without moving its controls, the tracks listed, a column added, and a control's cell shown.

**Decisions made while building step 7d-2** (the Free layout, 2026-10-30)
- **The design document** gains, on a group, `layout` (`flow`, the default, or `free`; `grid` comes with 7d-3) and `base_width` / `base_height`; on a control, `x` and `y` (0 to 4000 points from the top left of the group's inside) and `anchor_x` / `anchor_y` (`start`, the default, `end` or `both`). All are left out when not set.
- **Anchors need a size the places were made for.** A group's `base_width` (and `base_height`, only when it has a height) is the size of its inside when its places were last edited; drawn wider by *d*, a control anchored to the right moves by *d*, one anchored to both edges grows by *d* (it needs a width; choosing *Left and right* gives it the width it is drawn at), one anchored to the left stays. A group with no height is as tall as its lowest control (learnt in the frame before, so it settles in the second), so nothing is anchored to its bottom; the property page says so. `form::free_place` in `gossia-core` works out where a control is drawn, `form::rebase_free` makes the stored places the ones seen at the group's present size.
- **Every edit is made as seen.** A move, a resize in a Free group, the arrow keys, the align tools, the numbers and anchors on the property page each carry the size of the group's inside (`form_free::Placement`); the group is rebased to that size first, so a change made in a window of another size never makes the other controls jump.
- **Drawing.** The group takes the whole width; each control is drawn in a box of its own at its place (`Ui::new_child`), as wide as its width, or else up to the group's right edge; later controls are drawn over earlier ones. In layout mode the inside of a Free group is outlined and at least 48 points tall, so there is room to drop into it. Where each control and each Free group was drawn is kept from one frame to the next (`form_free::FormRects`) for the snapping, the arrow keys, the align tools and the property page.
- **Moving.** In a Free group a control is moved by dragging it (a group by its title); it is not dragged to another group, which *Move out of the group* and dragging into a group do. It follows the pointer from where it was, and its left, centre or right snaps to the left, centre or right of another control of the group or of the group, and its top, centre or bottom to theirs (and to the top of the group), within 5 points, with a guide line across the group; else its top left goes to the grid of 4 points. Alt moves it by whole points without snapping. One drag is one step of *Undo layout*, and it selects the control. Dragging from the palette, or a control of another group, into a Free group puts it at the end of the list (the end of the tab order) where it was let go, on the grid.
- **The arrow keys** move every selected control that is in a Free group by 1 point, or 4 with Shift; consecutive presses are one step of undo. They are read from the key events, with what was held with each.
- **Several controls.** Ctrl and a click adds a control to the selection or takes it out; the first selected is the one the others follow and the only one with handles. With two or more, the property page shows how many and the align tools: *Align left*, *right*, *top*, *bottom*, *centres across* and *down*, *Same width*, *Same height*, *Distribute across* and *down* (three or more; the outermost two stay, the gaps become equal). Moving tools need the controls in one Free group; *Same width* and *Same height* work in any layout. The context menu of a selected control has them in an *Align* menu. Each use is one change.
- **The property page** of a group chooses its *Layout*; a Flow group has *Columns*, a Free group lists its controls in **tab order**, with buttons that move one earlier or later (the group stays selected). Turning a group Free puts each control where it was drawn, from the top left of the first, on the grid, so nothing jumps. A control in a Free group has *Place in the group*, X and Y as seen, and *Anchored across* and *down*; its span and new row are not shown.
- **Not done:** moving several selected controls by dragging one (the arrow keys and the align tools move them all), and snapping while resizing to the edges of the others (resizing snaps to the grid).
- **Tests.** `gossia-core`: anchors at the base size and wider, rebasing keeping every control where it is seen, the new properties stored and checked. `form_free.rs` (unit): snapping to the grid, to an edge and to the group's centre, with the guides; aligning, same width and distributing; an align tool refused across groups and with too few controls. `tests/free_layout.rs`: controls drawn at their places and the group as tall as the lowest; a drag on the grid as one undo; snapping to another's edge; the arrow keys with and without Shift as one undo; Ctrl and a click and *Align left*; a right anchor following the width; turning a group Free without moving its controls, and the tab order; anchoring on the property page without moving; a label from the palette dropped at a point.

**Decisions made while building step 7e** (lists of choices, 2026-10-30)
- **One more expression of a control.** *Choices* is a fifth kind of control expression (`ExprKind::Choices` in `gossia-calc`), synced, run, made stale and trusted as the others are; on a column it runs for each row. It is not part of how a control is drawn (`Gate`): `form_exprs::choice_list` reads it for a field or a cell.
- **Items.** A list or tuple; each item a value, a pair `(code, text)` or a dict with `value` (and `label`, which may be left out or `None` for the code). A code and a text are scalars, written as a field shows them (`2`, `True`, `2026-10-30`). `None` is an empty list. Anything else is an error that names the first bad item ("item 1 is not a value, a (code, text) pair or a dict with value and label"), shown in red under *Choices* on the property page and on hover over the field, which is then a text box.
- **A code is the stored value** when it is the same text, the same number (`2` is the stored `2.0`) or the same truth, ignoring case. A chosen item is entered as the code typed would be (`FormAction::Commit`), so the entry path checks it against the type and *Undo* undoes it.
- **The dropdown** shows the code and the text of the stored item (`AMS — Amsterdam`; trailing spaces of a code are not shown, and a code of only spaces is `(spaces)`), or the stored value followed by *(not in the list)*, and offers *(none)* for an optional field. With *Allow other values*, *Other...* at the end of its list makes it a text box that starts on the code stored and has the focus; leaving it enters what was typed, read as the field's type, and Escape gives it up. **The typeahead** lists, while it has the focus, the items whose text contains what is typed, ignoring case: the text itself first, then texts that start with it, then a word that starts with it, then the rest, in the order of the list within each. Up and Down move the highlight, Enter takes it, a click takes an item (on the press, before the box loses the focus), Escape gives the typing up. Leaving the box otherwise takes the item whose text, else code, is what was typed, ignoring case; empty text clears an optional field; with *Allow other values* other text is entered as it is; without, it stays in the box with "“Paris” is not one of the choices" under it, and nothing is entered.
- **Not in the list.** A stored value that is not a code is kept and shown with *not in the list* beside the field (on hover in a cell of a table, which has no room).
- **The editor.** With choices, *Automatic* and *Dropdown list* are a dropdown, *Type to search* a typeahead, and *One line of text* or *Several lines of text* a text box of the code (the list is not used). A column offers the same, without several lines. Without choices, *Dropdown list* and *Type to search* are *Automatic*.
- **Enumerations.** On a node whose type is an enumeration, items whose code is not one of its values are left out of the dropdown and the typeahead; the property page says "2 items are not values of the enumeration and are left out" (for a column, of its first row).
- **Not run yet, untrusted.** Before the expression has first run the list is empty, with the pending marker; in a workbook that is not trusted the field is a text box, saying on hover that the workbook has to be trusted.
- **Tests.** `form_exprs.rs` (unit): items of each shape, wrong shapes named, the ranking of matches, an exact match by text then code, untrusted and failed lists. `tests/choices.rs`: a dropdown showing an item's text, storing its code, marking a value not in the list, and undone; a typeahead taking the second match with Down and Enter; taking typed text that is an item's text, and refusing other text; *Allow other values*; a list that reads another field following it, with an enumeration leaving an item out; untrusted, failed and wrongly shaped lists giving a text box; a column's choices for each row following the data; the property page setting the choices and counting what the enumeration leaves out. `gossia-core`: the new properties and editor values stored and read back, absent when not set. `gossia-calc`: the choices of a control and of a column are in the trust digest.

**Decisions made while building step 7d-1** (size and place of controls, 2026-10-30)
- **The design document** gains, on a control: `width` (24 to 2000 points), `fill`, `height` (16 to 2000), `align` (left, center, right), `label_position` (left, top, none), `label_width` (0 to 600), `span` (1 to 6), `new_row`, and on a group `spacing` and `padding` (0 to 48). All are left out when not set, so older designs read unchanged and a design that sets none of them is the same document as before. The version stays 1: a design written now that uses them is refused by an older build's schema check, which says which property it does not know.
- **A group of several columns is drawn as rows** (`form::flow_rows` in `gossia-core`), no longer as egui's independent columns: a control takes its span (at most the number of columns), and goes to a new row when it asks for one or does not fit; cells are of equal width with the group's spacing between them, and a row is as tall as its tallest control, so the controls of a row line up across columns. The generated form has no column groups, so it is unchanged; a customised form with columns now reads row by row, which is what the old drawing seemed to do but did not line up.
- **A control with a size or an alignment is drawn in a box of its own.** A width puts the control in a box that wide; `fill` uses the whole width of its cell. A field in such a box lets its editor fill what the label leaves (the usual editor stops at 360 points), keeping room for the *fx* and pending markers only when the field shows one. A height is the least height of the box, the height of a several-lines text box, a table or stacked repeater's height with a vertical scroll bar, a button's least height, and a group's least height inside its frame. Centring or right-aligning a control without a width uses the width it had when last drawn (egui knows a size only after drawing), so it settles in the second frame.
- **Labels.** A field's label is on the left at the label width (the field's own, else the group's, else 170 points), above the editor, or left out; a label width of 0 also leaves it out. The accessible name of the editor is the node's name either way.
- **The handles.** The selected control in layout mode has three square handles, on the middle of its right edge, the middle of its bottom edge and its bottom-right corner (a separator has only the right one). Dragging sets the width or height in points, measured from the control's left or top edge, snapped to 4 points unless Alt is held, within the ranges; the pointer shows the direction. A double click on a handle makes that size automatic again. Each drag is one step of *Undo layout*: a drag that starts bumps a counter, and the frames of the drag are merged into one change. A size set by a handle ends `fill`. The handles' identities do not depend on where the control is drawn, since a control that gets a width is drawn one box deeper.
- **The property page** has a *Size and place* section: *Fill the width*, *Width* and *Height* (each a tick box, automatic unless ticked, with the number in points beside it), *Alignment* in its cell, and as the kind allows the field's *Label* position and *Label width*, a group's *Label width* for its fields, *Spacing* and *Padding*, and in a group of several columns *Columns it takes* and *Start a new row*. Dragging a number is one change.
- **Not done in 7d-1:** the Free and Grid layouts, moving with the arrow keys, multiple selection and the align tools (7d-2 and 7d-3); a separate height for single-line text boxes (a height on a one-line field is the least height of its box only).
- **Tests.** `gossia-core`: rows from spans and new rows, a span wider than the group, and the new properties stored, read back and checked against their ranges. `form_layout_tests.rs`: a resize setting width and height and ending fill, the frames of one drag as one undo step, a size made automatic again, and the snapping. `tests/forms.rs`: the width handle dragged with real pointer events (snapped, drawn at that width, one undo, a double click resetting it), a group of two columns with a spanning first field lining the next two up side by side, a label above and a label left out, and the property page setting a width and a group's spacing.

**Decisions made for drag and drop in the form designer** (a leftover of step 7b, 2026-10-30)
- **What can be dragged.** In layout mode, a control (by its body, or a group by its title) and the buttons of the palette. A click still selects a control and still adds from the palette where the selection says; only a drag past egui's threshold drags. egui's own drag and drop carries the payload (what is dragged and its name), so Escape cancels a drag.
- **Where it goes.** While dragging, a line in the accent colour shows the place, and the name of what is dragged follows the pointer. Over a control, the upper half puts it before and the lower half after; a control counts across the whole width of its list, so the space beside a narrow label is the label's. Over a group, the top and bottom 6 points put it before or after the group, and the inside puts it among the group's controls (by which of their middles are above the pointer; an empty group takes it as its first). Anywhere else in the form, among the controls of the top level. Let go outside the form, nothing happens.
- **What is refused.** A group is not dropped into itself or into a group inside it, and a control put back where it is makes no change (no line is shown for either). Only a group takes controls, as with the menu commands.
- **One change.** A drop is one step of *Undo layout*, and selects the control where it landed. It is `FormDesign::move_to(from, parent, index)` in `gossia-core`, with the index counted as the list was before the move, or an insert of the palette's new control (`new_control`, the same as a click adds).
- **The menu commands stay** (Move up, Move down, into and out of a group), for the keyboard and for exact steps.
- **Not done:** dragging a field from *Fields not on the form* onto the form (it has *Add*); dragging between columns of a group by left and right (the place follows the vertical position only); scrolling the form while dragging near its edge.
- **Tests.** `gossia-core`: a control moved down and up its list, into a group after it, out to the end, and refused into itself or into what is not a group. `form_layout_tests.rs`: where a pointer puts a control (halves, group edges, inside a group, below everything, outside) and the refusals. `tests/forms.rs`: a field dragged after the next one with real pointer events over several frames, a label dragged from the palette, undone in one step, and a group dragged onto its own field changing nothing.

**Decisions made while building step 7c** (button procedures)
- **The call** is `Engine::call(&Call, ctx) -> Result<Vec<Write>, Failure>`, beside `eval`, so the language stays behind the engine trait. A `Call` is the procedure name, the tab, the name of the form, the label of the button and the time limit. A `Write` is a place and an operation: `Set(value)`, `Remove`, or `Append(value)` to an array. The engine gives the writes back and applies nothing; an engine without procedures fails with `#NAME!`.
- **Which function runs.** Only a public function of a workbook module: `name`, or `module.name` when two modules define the same name. A built-in, a tab, a module itself and a name that starts with an underscore are refused with *there is no procedure called ...*. A function of a module that failed to load says so.
- **`form` and `ctx`.** `form` is the proxy of the root of the tab, the same object a formula reaches by tab name. **Writing goes through the same proxies**: `form.customer.status = 'shipped'`, `form.items[0] = {...}`, `del form.note`, `form.items.append({...})`. A write to an object takes a field name, to an array an index that exists (`append` adds; an index past the end is refused), and `None` clears an optional field. While a procedure runs, `append` on an array is the method, not the field of its items. **A formula cannot write**: the proxies raise `TypeError` ("a formula cannot change data; only a button procedure can") unless a procedure's list of writes is open. `ctx` has `form` (the name of the form), `button` (its label) and `tab(name)`, which gives the proxy of another tab, for reading and writing. Return values are ignored.
- **What a procedure reads** is the data as it was when it started. **It does not see its own writes**: `form.count = 10` then `form.count` still reads the old value, since the writes are collected and applied after the call. A procedure that needs a running value keeps it in a variable.
- **Applying** is `Calc::apply_procedure`, on the thread that owns the workbook. Every write is checked against the schema of its place (`validate_at`, as an entry is) and refused if it is in the result of a formula or on a formula; removing a required field is refused (an item of an array, or an optional field, may go). The writes run inside one entry of the history, `Run <button>`; if any is refused, the entry is taken back (`History::abort`) and leaves nothing to undo or redo, and the message names the place and the reason. Writes are applied in the order they were made, so an append followed by a write to the new item works.
- **The conflict rule** is the one for an evaluation that an edit overtook: if a node the procedure wrote, or one above or below it, was edited after the procedure started (`edited_since` its version), none of the writes are applied and the message names the node. An edit to another node does not refuse them.
- **When it runs.** Clicking asks the session (`Session::run_procedure`). The call waits until nothing is out of date and nothing is running, then takes a fresh snapshot, so it reads what is on the screen with every formula brought up to date. Only one is waiting or running at a time; another click is refused with a message, and undo and redo (the keys and the menu items) do nothing in the meantime. In an untrusted workbook `run_procedure` refuses, and a procedure that was waiting when trust was taken back finishes with a message and does not run.
- **The thread.** The evaluation thread takes `Job::Evaluate` and `Job::Call`; a call comes back as `ProcedureResult` (`Evaluator::take_calls`). It has its own limit, `EvalSettings::procedure_limit` (10 s, the design's button procedure time limit), given to the engine's watchdog for that call; a procedure that does not stop within the limit and the grace period is abandoned like a formula, with a message and no writes. `Session::take_finished` gives the outcomes (`Finished { call, outcome }`), and `Status::procedure` is the label of the button that is waiting or running, which the status bar shows as `Running <label>...`.
- **The button in the form.** A click first enters what is being typed (the text of the cell being edited and every draft of the form; a refused entry stops the click and shows why), then asks for the procedure. A button is disabled, with the reason as its tip, when it has no procedure, when the workbook is not trusted, and while any procedure waits or runs (so all the buttons of the workbook are disabled). A failure, or a refused write, shows as `<button>: <message>` in the line above the form, and the data is as it was. In layout mode buttons are drawn disabled, as before.
- **Control channel:** `click_button {label}` clicks the button of that label on the form shown, and `status` reports `procedure`; `wait_idle` waits for a procedure as it does for evaluation and takes its outcome into `notice`.
- **Not done in this step, and why.** *Visible when* and *enabled when* are still stored and edited but not evaluated: they are expressions on controls with dependencies, results kept in memory and the pending marker, which is a second use of the evaluator and is the next part of the form work *(done later: see the decisions for expressions on controls)*. The property page offers a text box for the procedure, not a list of the functions of the modules *(done later: see the decisions for the procedure list)*. The Immediate window and data source writes (6b) are not here. A procedure's writes through `append` and a numbered index only reach arrays that exist; making an absent optional array or object is `Set` of the whole value.
- **Tests.** In `gossia-calc`, `procedure_tests.rs`, 16 against the real interpreter: the writes as one undoable entry; reading the data as it was and knowing the form and the button; writing to another tab through `ctx`; a write that does not fit refusing all of them and leaving no entry; an exception; a computed node; removing an optional field and refusing a required one; replacing and removing items; which names can be procedures; a formula that tries to write; waiting for the workbook to be up to date; one at a time; an untrusted workbook; the time limit; and the conflict rule both ways. In `gossia-app`, `tests/buttons.rs`, 9 that drive the window (a click and its one undo, a field typed but not entered, an exception shown, a refused write, a missing procedure, untrusted and no-procedure buttons disabled, undo waiting, the control channel) and one on the status text.

**Decisions made while building step 8a and 8b** (the workbook layer, files)
- **Entries are made in memory.** `gossia-calc` builds and reads a `Package`, an ordered list of `(path, bytes)`; `gossia-app` turns it into a zip and into a file. The calculation crate still touches no filesystem. `Calc::to_package(&Meta)` and `Calc::from_package(syntax, &Package)` are the two ends; `Meta` is what the application adds (workbook id, application version, the state of each tab's view, and the entries this version does not know).
- **The layout is the design's**, with the entries in a fixed order (manifest, then each tab's data, overlay and forms in tab order, then the modules, then unknown entries in path order), JSON pretty-printed with a stable key order, LF, UTF-8, a trailing newline, and the zip written with one fixed time (1 January 1980), fixed permissions and one compression level. Saving what has not changed gives the same bytes (tested at both levels). The overlay is left out when a tab has no formulas and no view state.
- **The manifest** records `format_version` (`1.0`), `app_version`, `workbook_id`, `reserved_names_version`, the engine (`rustpython` and the revision pinned in `vendor.lock`, which is a constant to keep in step with it), the tabs in order (name, kind `data`, schema binding `builtin:jsonschema`, `builtin:xsd` or `sheet:<Tab>`, `auto_update` when on, and the forms in display order) and the modules in order.
- **Reading is in two passes**: schema tabs first, so that the tabs bound to them can be typed, then the rest; then the tabs are put in manifest order, auto-update is switched on (it needs the tabs that use the schema to be there), the forms are read and the modules added. Nothing is run and nothing is evaluated. Afterwards nothing is out of date, there is nothing to undo, and the workbook counts as saved; the caller decides about trust and then calls `recalc_all` if the workbook is trusted.
- **Stored errors.** The data file leaves an errored node out; the overlay keeps the error (code, message, origin, traceback) and reading puts the error value back into the node, so an untrusted workbook shows its errors without running anything. A formula that does not parse comes back as `#SYNTAX!`. A formula saved while out of date is saved as `pending`.
- **Versions.** The same major version opens; a newer minor version opens with a warning and keeps what it does not know (including `settings.json`, `datasources.json` and `reports/`, which this version does not read yet and writes back unchanged); a newer major version is refused with the version it needs. A different engine revision gives a warning.
- **A file that breaks the rules still opens, with a warning**, for a tab name that is not a valid name (such as `Orders schema`), a form that cannot be read (dropped), a module that cannot be added, and a formula the schema cannot hold (dropped). It is refused for what cannot be repaired: no manifest, an unreadable manifest, a tab name that is not safe as a file name or two that differ only in case, a missing data file, and a tab whose schema tab is missing or cannot be used as a type.
- **Safety of reading.** `Package::insert` refuses a path that is empty, absolute, has `..` or `.` or an empty part, a backslash, a colon or a control character. The zip reader (`file.rs`) limits the entries (10,000), the bytes of an entry (512 MiB) and the total (1 GiB), reads at most one byte beyond the limit whatever the header says, and refuses duplicates and encrypted entries. These are constants until the preferences exist. The `zip` crate is used with only `deflate-flate2` (pure Rust, no encryption, no C library).
- **Saving** waits for outstanding evaluation, writes to a temporary file beside the target and renames it over the original, then marks the workbook saved. *Save As* with a new path gives the workbook a new id and carries the trust of the original; saving to the same path keeps the id. A tab that was given its type directly (no schema tab) cannot be saved, which only the tests can make.
- **Unsaved changes** are the history's modified flag, or a change to the forms (`Calc::forms_version`, because form designs are not in the undo history). The window title is the file name, `*` when there are unsaved changes, and `- Gossia`. *Open...*, *Quit* and the window's close button ask *Save the changes?* (Save, Don't save, Cancel); a workbook with no name goes through *Save As* first.
- **Trust on opening.** The id and the content digest are looked up in the trust record: a match, or a workbook with no formulas and no modules, opens trusted and is calculated; an id that is not recorded opens untrusted with the banner; an id that is recorded with other content opens untrusted and the banner lists what changed. Saving a trusted workbook records its digest. The record is in memory for now.
- **The file browser is `egui-file-dialog`** (MIT; egui only, so no GTK, portal or native dialog is needed, and it works on the runner), vendored from the branch that moves it to egui 0.37 (`vendor.lock`, pinned to a commit; its release 0.15.0 is for egui 0.36; switch to the crates.io release when one for 0.37 exists). *Open...*, *Save As...* and Ctrl+O, Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Shift+S open it, with a `.gossia` filter or save extension and the folder of the workbook as the start. Its answer goes through the same `open_path` and `save_as`; if that fails, the text dialog opens with the chosen path and the reason. The text dialog is also what the commands open when `files.browse` is off, which is how the tests type a name. The browser was written with emoji and symbols that the window's fonts lack, and egui 0.37 stops on a character no font has, so its labels and icons are replaced with the Phosphor icons (the font is now also a fallback of the text families, and a character nothing has is drawn as a box); the three symbols it draws without a setting (the OK and cancel marks of its *new folder* and rename dialogs) show as boxes.
- **Control channel:** `open_workbook {path}`, `save`, `save_as {path}` (and `file` and `modified` in `status`) work; `new_workbook`, `close`, `menu`, `context_menu`, `set_option` and `grant` still say they are not supported yet (`new_workbook` came with 8c and `set_option` with 8g).
- **Not done, and why.** The state of each tab's view is carried through the file unchanged but nothing produces or reads it yet (8c); the trust record and preferences are not on disk (8c); `New` waits for a way to create a tab; formulas read from a file have no recorded dependencies until they run, so in an untrusted workbook an edit does not yet mark their dependents stale; a tab whose schema tab cannot be used as a type refuses the file instead of opening flagged.
- **Tests.** In `gossia-calc`, 18 on the package (round trip of JSON and XML tabs with formulas, forms and modules; identical bytes; the fixed order; the manifest; errors, syntax errors and pending formulas; carried view state and unknown entries; versions; unsafe names and paths; missing files; warnings). In `gossia-app`, 7 on the zip and the atomic save, 15 without a window on opening, saving, trust, the title, the dialogs and the control channel, and 5 that drive the window (*Save As*, *Open* with the question, cancelling, quitting, and the banner for a file not seen before).

**Decisions made while building column formulas**
- **A rule on top of ordinary formulas.** `Calc` keeps, per tab, a list of rules (`ColumnFormula`: the array, the field, the text), and every item of the array has an ordinary formula on that field with the rule's text, so evaluation, dependencies, errors, display and `parent` are the ones item formulas already have. The rule is what makes a new item get its formula and what is saved. `Calc::set_column_formula`, `remove_column_formula`, `column_formulas`, `column_formula`, `column_of`.
- **Every edit that can add or remove items keeps the items in step** (insert, remove, setting a value on the array or an item): the items that lack the formula get it and a formula past the end of the array is dropped, as operations of the same history entry, so one undo takes an inserted item and its formula back.
- **Overriding one item** (a literal, a different formula, clearing the formula, removing the field) ends the rule in the same entry, and the other items keep their formulas as ordinary ones. Entering the same text again changes nothing.
- **One entry in the history** each for creating, changing and removing a column formula (an `Op::Column` and the item formulas it made); undo puts back the rule, the old formulas and the values the field held.
- **Removing it keeps the values** the formulas produced.
- **Where it can be used:** a field of the items themselves (not inside an object of the items: `items[].price.net` is refused), of an array that is not inside another array (`orders[].lines[].total` is refused), where the schema allows a formula and nothing above the array is computed. These are the open points of the specification, left refused until they are needed.
- **Saved once.** The overlay has a `columns` list (array path, field, text); the items that are as the rule makes them have no entry, and an item whose formula ended in an error keeps an entry without text (status and error only). Reading gives every item without an entry the formula, marks nothing out of date and runs nothing. Saving what was read gives the same bytes.
- **The trust record** has one item for the column (`column formula Orders!items[].total`) and none for its items.
- **In the window:** a click on a header of the tabular view **selects the column** (outlined header, tinted cells; the name box shows `items[].total`), and the formula bar shows `=` and the rule, or nothing; typing a formula and pressing Enter sets it, and clearing the bar removes it. Anything but a formula is refused with the reason. Fields inside objects of the items and summary columns say why they cannot have one. **Sorting moved to the header's context menu** (Sort ascending, Sort descending), because the click is the selection now; the form tables still sort on click. The sample's three item totals are now one column formula.
- **Not done:** a mark on the cells of a column that tells they belong to a formula (they have the computed tint), the forms setting a column formula, and a column formula for a nested array.

**Decisions made while building step 8c** (what a file carries besides the tabs, and what the user keeps apart from it)
- **Workbook settings** are `WorkbookSettings` in `gossia-calc` (`settings.rs`): the settings of the table under Settings and limits (eight, and the two limits of reports since 12e), each with its key, default, default ceiling and smallest value (1, or 0 for the grace period). Times are whole milliseconds and sizes whole bytes, so the keys say so (`formula_time_limit_ms`, `max_response_bytes`).
- **`settings.json` holds only what differs from the defaults**, in the order of the table, then the keys this version does not know, in key order, which are kept and written back. A workbook with default settings has no `settings.json`, so its files stay as they were, and a later change of a default reaches it. A value that cannot be used (not a whole number, below the smallest value) is left at its default, with a warning; a file that is not a JSON object is the defaults, with a warning. Either way the workbook opens.
- **Trust.** The settings are one item of the digest, `workbook settings`, when they are not the defaults. So a workbook with no formulas and no modules still has no executable content and opens trusted, and a settings file edited outside the application is listed in the banner like a changed formula.
- **Changing the settings** is `Calc::set_settings`, one entry of the history (`Change the workbook settings`). Nothing in the window calls it yet: that is the Options dialog. Undo and redo put the settings back, and the window passes the new values on (below).
- **Ceilings and what is in force.** `Ceilings` is the user's maximum for each setting, and `WorkbookSettings::effective` gives each setting held down to its ceiling. Opening a file whose settings a ceiling holds down says so in the notice (*The workbook asks for a formula time limit of 5000; your ceiling of 2000 is used.*).
- **The evaluation thread follows the settings.** The window compares what is in force with what the thread was last given, every frame, and on a change calls `Evaluator::reconfigure` with a new engine factory (for the `range` guard) and new limits. The thread's interpreter was made with the old ones, so the thread is retired: at once if it is idle, otherwise when what it runs comes back, and the next evaluation starts a fresh one, which loads the modules again, as it does after an abandoned thread. The data source settings are kept and checked but not used until data sources exist (6b).
- **The user's files** (`prefs.rs` in `gossia-app`) are `preferences.json` and `trust.json`, in `%APPDATA%\Gossia` on Windows, `~/Library/Application Support/Gossia` on macOS and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/gossia` (or `~/.config/gossia`) elsewhere, or in the folder named by `GOSSIA_CONFIG_DIR`, which the end-to-end tests set so that they never touch the user's own. Two files rather than one, so that a change to the recent files does not rewrite the trust record. Both are versioned (`"version": 1`); a newer version is read for what this one knows, everything it does not know (top-level keys and keys inside the sections) is written back, and the higher version number is kept. A missing file is the defaults and is not created until something changes. A file that cannot be read is renamed to `*.json.unreadable` with a warning, so that writing the defaults later does not destroy it.
- **What the preferences hold now:** the ceilings, the limits for reading a workbook (zip entries, total and per-entry size), the bounds of the undo history, and the recent files. Every known value is written, so the file shows the user what can be set. The appearance, display, editor and import preferences of the Options dialog arrive with it.
- **Another window may write the files.** Every change reads the file again, makes the change, and writes it atomically, so two windows that each trust a workbook keep both decisions. The change holds in memory even if writing fails, and the notice says so.
- **When trust is recorded:** on saving a trusted workbook (as before), and now also on *Trust this version*, so that a workbook trusted from the banner opens trusted the next time even if it is not saved. *Save As* of a workbook that is not trusted carries the original's record to the copy's new id, so the copy opens with the same list of changes.
- **The view state** of a tab is the `view` object of its overlay (`view_state.rs`): `show` (`"raw"` or `{"form": name}`, left out for the data tree), `tables` (one entry per array shown as a table, in path order, with `hidden_columns`, `hidden_rows` and `filters`), and `table`, the one in the pane. Rows hidden by hand are saved by position and become the items at those positions when the file is read, so they stay with their items through a sort as before. Filters are saved with their condition and operands as typed. Changing view state is not an unsaved change, as it is not an edit; it is written with the next save. Keys of the object this version does not know are kept. State that no longer fits (a form or an array that is gone, a row past the end) is dropped without a warning, since it changes only what is drawn. Which nodes are open in the tree is not saved.
- **On start** Gossia opens a blank workbook, the one *File > New* makes, or the workbook named on the command line *(owner, 2026-11-04; it opened the Orders and Prices sample before)*. The sample is still what most window tests start from (`GossiaApp::sample`). The window driven by the end-to-end tests (the `test-control` build) starts blank like any other: the shared control scenario drops `Orders.json` on it with the new `drop` command (as dragging the file onto the window does, pressing Import in each import dialog with its choices as they are), enters the subtotal formula itself, and then edits and recalculates; its in-process run starts from the blank workbook too.
- **File > New** (Ctrl+N), after the question about unsaved changes, makes a workbook with a data tab `Data` bound to a schema tab `DataSchema` whose record has no fields yet (`{"type": "object", "properties": {}}`), with auto-update on, so that the fields are added from the data tab with the commands of 4d. It has no file, a new id, nothing to undo, and is trusted, having no executable content. Making tabs of other kinds, and adding tabs to a workbook, wait for a design of their own; import (a later step) is the other way tabs arrive. The application still starts with the sample.
- **Recent files** are the ten workbooks opened or saved last, newest first, as absolute paths, in the preferences; the same file is listed once (ignoring case on Windows). *File > Open Recent* lists them by name, with the folder on hover, and *Clear the list*; choosing one asks about unsaved changes first. A file that is no longer there is taken off the list when it is chosen, and the notice says why it did not open.
- **Control channel:** `new_workbook` works (it does not ask about unsaved changes, as `open_workbook` does not), and `status` reports `recent_files`.
- **Not done, and why.** Nothing edits the workbook settings or the preferences in the window yet, apart from the recent files: that is the Options dialog, as is the list of trusted workbooks with *Revoke* (`TrustStore::revoke` exists). The import limits (file size, nesting depth) come with import. *Save and restart* comes with the Options dialog.
- **Tests.** In `gossia-calc`, 9 on the settings (`settings_tests.rs`: the file, only what differs, unknown keys, values that cannot be used, a file that cannot be read, undo and redo, the digest, ceilings, the defaults of the design) and 2 on reconfiguring the evaluation thread, idle and while running. In `gossia-app`, 8 on the user's files (`prefs.rs`: defaults with no files, trust kept for the next session, two windows, unknown keys and a newer version kept, values that cannot be used, a file moved aside, the recent list, memory only) and 15 without a window (`workbook_state_tests.rs`: view state saved and restored with the same bytes, hidden rows after a sort, a form view and one that is gone, unknown view keys; trust from a save and from the banner read by a later session, a copy that is not trusted; settings reaching the thread and back on undo, a ceiling on opening, reading limits from the preferences; *New*, its question, and the control channel; the recent list kept on disk, and a file that is gone). The shared control scenario now ends with `new_workbook`, in process and on the running application.

**Decisions made while building the look and feel**
- **Changed from the Canva token list:** in the light theme, the selection is stronger (`#CCE5FA`) and the computed tint greyer, because the two were nearly the same blue and a computed cell looked selected; the stale text is darker (`#7A838D`), because the first value was faint on white. The computed tint is translucent in both themes rather than a solid colour, so that a selected computed cell still shows the selection. The body and data text stays at the 14 points of the Options dialog's default rather than the mockup's 13. A *primary* colour was added for the main button (white text on the accent was too faint in the dark theme).
- **Text fields have a 1 px border** at rest, which egui takes from the style of an inactive widget, so buttons have it too; without it a field on a bar of the same colour could not be seen.
- **The bars** (menu, formula bar, schema bar, sheet tabs, status bar) are in the chrome colour, the grid and the panels in the panel colour, and the trust banner fills its whole bar. The name box of the formula bar is a field-like box, left-aligned, in the monospace font.
- **Sheet tabs** are drawn by the window: the current one in the panel colour with an accent line along its top and medium text, the others in weak text with a hover fill. They report themselves as tabs to screen readers.
- **Fonts are part of the binary** (about 3 MB, through `include_bytes!`), like the icons. They are pinned to a commit of the Google Fonts repository, with their checksums, in `assets/fonts/FONTS.md`.
- **Not done:** the toolbar and node inspector of the mockup (new features, not styling; done later, see the decisions for the toolbar and the node inspector), a 2 px focus outline on the selected tree cell, and the issues panel as styled rows with icons. The Linux snapshot images must be made again on the runner.
- **Tests.** 3 in `style.rs`: every family is made of fonts that exist and ends with the icons, every bundled font has a weight axis, and the computed tint is translucent and not the selection. Later: an icon is drawn from the icon font in every family, and taking out the private use area keeps every other character of Inter. The picture test takes `GOSSIA_THEME=light`.

**Decisions made while building the toolbar and the node inspector** (step 9, 2026-10-30)
- **What the toolbar has**, from the mockup (Open, Save, Validate, Beautify, Add node, Insert function, the theme) fitted to what Gossia does. *New* was added, and *Undo* and *Redo*. *Validate* became the Issues button, since validation runs all the time: the button opens the panel, and its icon says whether there is anything in it. *Beautify* was left out: it formats JSON text, and only the Raw view shows text, which is laid out already. *Add node* became *Add an item*: with an array selected it adds an item at the end; with an item, or anything inside one, it adds one after that item, as the row commands do. Fields are added from the context menu, which asks for a name.
- **Each button does what its menu command does** and is enabled when that command is: *Save*, *Undo* and *Redo* wait while a cell is being edited, and *Undo* and *Redo* while a button procedure runs. *Add an item* and *Insert a function* work in the data tree only. The tool buttons have names of their own (*New workbook*, *Save workbook*, *Show issues*, *Show node inspector*), so that a screen reader, and a test, tells them from the menu items.
- **The theme buttons** set the same preference as the Options dialog. The one in force is shown pressed; clicking it again goes back to following the system, which is the default and shows neither pressed.
- **The icons** are Phosphor's: file-plus, folder-open, floppy-disk, the arc arrows for undo and redo, plus, check-circle or warning, a panel with a sidebar, sun and moon. Their code points were read from the bundled font by drawing it.
- **The inspector works out its facts without a window** (`GossiaApp::inspect`, a list of a label, a text and a tone), so that tests read what it says; the panel draws them as a grid, paths and formulas in the monospace font, problems in the error colour, states in the warning colour. *Allowed* is the schema's rules in words: number bounds (`from 0 to 1`), the choices of an enum, string lengths, a pattern, a date format, and an array's number of items with the rule for each item. *Problem* lists a scalar's own validation errors, or for a container only those at the node itself; a required node that is absent is one. A formula's result that the schema refuses is an error value (`#TYPE!`), so it is shown as the error.
- **Not in the inspector:** which formulas read the node and which it reads, since the calculation keeps them only as an index from paths to readers; a form view (the inspector is for the data tree; a form has its property page).
- **The toolbar and the inspector are not saved**, as the Issues panel is not: the toolbar is shown and the inspector hidden when the window opens, and *File > New* and *Open* keep them as they are.
- **Every snapshot changed**, since the toolbar is a new bar at the top of the window. The Linux images must be made again on the runner.
- **Tests.** `tests/toolbar.rs`: *Undo* disabled with nothing to undo, then undo and redo of an entry; *Add an item* disabled outside an array, at the end of the selected array and after the selected item; the Issues panel, the inspector with its copy button, and the theme from the toolbar, back to the system on a second click; *View > Toolbar*; and what the inspector says of a formula, a value with bounds, a column formula, an enum, an array, an absent field and an error, and of data from a file that breaks its rules or lacks a required field.

**Decisions made while adding the choice of font**
- **The data font belongs to the workbook**, not to the user: it replaces the *data font size* that the Options dialog was to have. It is view state, like the view of each tab: changing it is not an edit (no undo, no unsaved-changes mark), it is written with the next save, and it is not part of the trust digest. It is kept in a new entry, `view.json`, at the root of the archive (`{"data_font": {"family": "serif", "size": 16}}`), written only when something differs from the defaults. A file without it opens in Sans 14; a value that cannot be used (an unknown typeface, a size outside 6 to 72) is the default; a `view.json` that is not a JSON object opens with the defaults and a warning; keys this version does not know are kept. An older build keeps `view.json` as an unknown entry, so the format version is unchanged. *File > New* starts in the default font.
- **What the data font covers:** the tree grid and the tabular pane below it, and the Raw view. Column titles, the formula bar, menus and bars keep the interface font, so the window's chrome does not change size. The type badges and the formulas in the grid follow the data font (first built with both in fixed fonts, which looked broken when the rest of the row changed): a badge takes the typeface (medium Inter under Sans) and is scaled from 11 points as the body is from 14; code (formulas, and the body of the Raw view) takes the typeface, except under Sans, where it stays in Geist Mono, the font chosen to go with Inter. Rows grow with the font.
- **The typefaces are one list in `gossia-core`** (`form::FontFamily`, which the app calls `Typeface`), since the form schema names them; `style::family` maps each to its bundled font. A form control's `font` and `font_size` are applied with `style::use_font`, which sets every text style of the control's `Ui` from the defaults (so a size set inside a group with a size does not compound).
- **Tests.** In `gossia-core`, the font properties in the schema and their bounds. In `gossia-calc`, `view.json` written only when not empty, read back, not unknown, and refused with a warning when it is not an object. In `gossia-app`: `style::use_font` sets every style, code included, and does not compound; a badge grows with the font of its `Ui`; the data font saved, reopened, not an edit, not in the digest, and reset by *New*; an unusable font and unknown keys in `view.json`; *View > Font* in the window, with taller rows; a group's font inherited by its controls and a control's own size; and the font chosen on the property page.

**Decisions made while adding table columns and number and date formats**
- **One list in screen order**, `Control.table_columns`, holds the order, the visibility and the properties of the columns, with the row numbers as an entry named `#` so that they are a column like the others (a field of the items named `#` would be shadowed by them). `Control::arrange_columns` gives the columns as the screen has them: those listed that the items have, in order; `#` first if it is not listed; then the fields not listed, shown. A listed name the items do not have is not drawn but is kept, so a field that is renamed back gets its properties back. `Control::set_columns` stores a list that is the default (every column shown, in the schema's order, with no properties) as nothing.
- **The first designs' `show`** (the columns shown, in order, the others hidden) is still read; the first change to the columns writes `table_columns` instead and drops `show`. The design version stays 1.
- **A column is selected through its control.** Layout mode keeps the selected control and, beside it, the name of a column of its table; selecting anything else clears it. Changes to columns are design edits like any other, each one step of *Undo layout*, and typing into a column's title or help is one step. The column page has *Back to the table*.
- **Row numbers** are stored as `numbering` (`index`, `decimal`, `lower-alpha`, `upper-alpha`, `lower-roman`, `upper-roman`) and `start` on the `#` column, each left out when it is the default; a start that was not set follows the numbering when it changes (0 for the index, 1 otherwise), and one that was set is kept. They only label the rows: sorting, moving and the paths are unchanged, and the row's menu is the same.
- **The title of a column in layout mode** spans the column, or its title where that is wider (an automatic column, the row numbers): the cell's `max_rect` joined with the space its title used. The response of the cell is not used, since it reaches past the column by the spacing, into the next title.
- **Title fonts belong to the table** (`title_font`, `title_font_size`, `header_font`, `header_font_size` on the repeater), and a column's `font` is the font of its cells only: the titles of a table are one row, and read best in one font. The header row is as tall as its font needs, and the rows as tall as the largest cell font. A column's title has its own alignment (`title_align`), apart from the cells' (`align`), so a column of numbers can have its cells on the right and its title on either side.
- **The column page offers only one-line editors** (automatic, or one line of text): a cell of a table has the height of a row, so several lines do not fit.
- **Formats are in `gossia-core`** (`format.rs`, with `form::Format` for a control's or a column's three properties), English month and day names, and are applied by the form only.
